is a LASSO estimator in a high-dimensional linear model. We will make use below of the fact that its asymptotic theory is well understood.

Furthermore, we assume that
\widehat{\gamma}_{n}
can be approximated by the minimizer
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n}
of

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(Y_{n,i,t}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\widetilde{C}_{n,ij}g(\gamma,Z_{n,ij,t})\right)^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widetilde{C}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1},

where
C_{ij}
is replaced by the oracle estimators
\widetilde{C}_{n,ij}
. That bias and standard deviation are of the same order up to logarithmic terms is caused by optimal choices of penalty constants which balance these two terms.

We now consider the estimators
(\widehat{C}_{n,ij},\widehat{\gamma}_{n})
assuming that the above approximations are valid and that for each
1\leq i\leq n
the parameters
C^{*}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}=(C^{*}_{n,ij}:j=1,...,n)
are sparse with
\big|\{j:C^{*}_{n,ij}\not=0\}\big|
bounded by a fixed constant. Then, up to a logarithmic factor, the bias of
\widetilde{C}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}=(\widetilde{C}_{n,ij}:j=1,...,n)
is of order
O(T^{-1/2})
. Furthermore the variance is of order
O(T^{-1})
.

Using expansions of the function
\gamma\to g(\gamma,z))
about
\gamma=\gamma_{n}^{*}
we obtain that, approximately,
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n}
is given as the sum of functions of
\widetilde{C}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}
. The summands are independent because
the tuples
\widetilde{C}_{n,i,.}
are independent and for each
i
given as the minimizer of

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(Y_{n,i,t}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{ij}g(\gamma_{n}^{*},Z_{n,ij,t})\right)^{2}+2\omega_{i}\|C_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}.

We conclude that up to a logarithmic factor
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n}
has a bias of order
O(T^{-1/2})
whereas the variance is of order
O((nT)^{-1})
. Because the size of the bias is not of order
O((nT)^{-1/2})

the estimator
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n}
does not achieve the rate
O_{P}((nT)^{-1/2})
.

The situation changes if we replace
\widetilde{C}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}
by bias corrected estimators as discussed e.g. in
van de Geer et al. (2014)
. Then the bias of the estimators is up to logarithmic terms of order
O(T^{-1})
which results in a bias of order
O(T^{-1})
for the estimator of
\gamma
which is of order
o((nT)^{-1/2})
as long as
T/n\to\infty
. Our discussion on the sufficiency of this rate heavily depends on the assumption that the approximations of the debiased estimator
\widehat{\gamma}_{n}
by
\widetilde{\gamma}_{n}
are valid for this rate of convergence. In the proof of Theorem
3.6
we use the stronger assumption
T/n^{3}\to\infty
which may be avoided at the cost of more complex calculations.

Appendix E
Discussion of the random compatibility condition

We argue in this section that (RCC) can be expected to hold in a slightly simplified setup. Specifically, we assume for now that the infimum in the definition of
\phi_{\text{comp}}^{2}
is only taken over
C
and that
\theta=\theta_{n}^{*}
and
\alpha=\alpha_{n}^{*}
are fixed to the true parameters. Moreover, we assume that
\alpha_{n}^{*}\geq\alpha_{0}>0
is uniformly bounded from below. First, we rewrite

\frac{1}{nT}\left\|\Psi_{n}(\cdot;C,\alpha_{n}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})-\lambda_{n}(\cdot)\right\|_{T}^{2}=\frac{1}{nT}\left\langle(C-C_{n}^{*})g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*}),(C-C_{n}^{*})g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\right\rangle_{T},

where, for matrix valued functions
A,B:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
,

\langle A,B\rangle_{T}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t-}A_{ij}(t-r)dN_{n,j}(r)\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t-}B_{ij}(t-r)dN_{n,j}(r)dt.

We apply next Proposition 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
. For the convenience of the reader, we reformulate the above in the notation of
Hansen et al. (2015)
. Let to this end,

Z_{C}(N_{n}):=\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}(C_{ij}-C_{n,ij}^{*})\int_{-A}^{0-}g(-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{n,j}(r)\right)^{2},

and let
\mathcal{S}_{t}
denote the shift-operator such that

\frac{1}{nT}\langle(C-C_{n}^{*})g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*}),(C-C_{n}^{*})g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\rangle_{T}=\int_{0}^{T}\left(Z_{C}\circ\mathcal{S}_{t}\right)(N_{n})dt.

It is now direct to see that

\displaystyle Z_{C}(N_{n})\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{n}^{2}}{T}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{\infty}^{2}\max_{j=1,...,n}|N_{n,j}[-A,0)|^{2}\leq\frac{c_{n}^{2}}{T}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{\infty}^{2}(1+|N_{n}[-A,0)|^{2}).

(47)

In other words, the condition of Proposition 3 holds for
b=\frac{c_{n}^{2}}{T}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{\infty}^{2}
and
\eta=2
. Furthermore, we have to study the quantity

\sigma_{n}^{2}:=\mathbb{E}\left(\left(Z_{C}(N_{n})-\mathbb{E}(Z_{C}(N_{n}))\right)^{2}\mathbbm{1}(|N_{n}[-A,0)|\leq C_{3}\log T)\right),

where
C_{3}>0
will be chosen later. Noe that, by Equation (
25
), we have, for specific choices of
a,c_{0}>0
, that for all
x>0

\mathbb{P}(|N_{n,i}[-A,0)|\geq x)\leq c_{0}\exp\left(-\frac{ax}{3}\right)
. Using this result and Equation (
47
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{C}(N_{n})^{2}\mathbbm{1}(|N_{n}[-A,0)|\leq C_{3}\log T))\leq\frac{c_{n}^{4}\log^{4}T}{T^{2}}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{\infty}^{4}C_{3}^{4}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(Z_{C}(N_{n}))\leq\frac{c_{n}^{2}}{T}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\max_{j=1,...,n}|N_{n,j}[-A,0)|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{n}^{2}}{T}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{\infty}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{j=1,...,n}|N_{n,j}[-A,0)|\geq\sqrt{t}\right)dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{c_{n}^{2}}{T}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(\frac{9}{a^{2}}\log^{2}n+n\int_{\frac{9}{a^{2}}\log^{2}n}^{\infty}c_{0}e^{-\frac{a\sqrt{t}}{3}}dt\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{n}^{2}}{T}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(\frac{9}{a^{2}}\log^{2}n-n\frac{18c_{0}}{a^{2}}e^{-\sqrt{x}}(\sqrt{x}+1)\big|_{\log^{2}n}^{\infty}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{n}^{2}}{T}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{\infty}^{2}\left(\frac{9}{a^{2}}\log^{2}n+n\frac{18c_{0}}{a^{2}}\frac{1}{n}(\log n+1)\right).

The above implies that

\displaystyle\sigma_{n}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\mathbb{E}\left(Z_{C}(N_{n})^{2}\mathbbm{1}(|N_{n}[-A,0)|\leq C_{3}\log T)\right)+\mathbb{E}(Z_{C}(N_{n}))^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{c_{n}^{4}}{T^{2}}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{\infty}^{4}\left(C_{3}^{4}\log^{4}T+\left(\frac{9}{a^{2}}\log^{2}n+\frac{18c_{0}}{a^{2}}(\log n+1\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\approx\frac{c_{n}^{4}}{T^{2}}(\log^{4}T+\log^{4}n),

where
\approx
is hiding some constants. In particular, when assuming

\frac{c_{n}^{2}}{\sqrt{T}}\left(\log^{2}T+\log^{2}n\right)\log^{\frac{3}{2}}T\to 0,

we have that
\sigma_{n}\sqrt{T\log^{3}T}\to 0
. Since also
b_{n}\log^{4}T\to 0
, we may conclude from Proposition 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
that

\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{C\in[0,K_{C})^{n\times n}}\left|\frac{1}{nT}\left\|\Psi_{n}(\cdot;C,\alpha_{n}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})-\lambda_{n}(\cdot)\right\|_{T}^{2}-T\mathbb{E}(Z_{C}(N_{n}))\right|>\varepsilon\right)\to 0,

for all
\varepsilon>0
. In the notation of Section 5.1 in
Hansen et al. (2015)
, we have that

\displaystyle T\mathbb{E}\left(Z_{C}(N_{n})\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\left(\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}(C_{ij}-C_{n,ij}^{*})\int_{-A}^{0-}g(-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{n,j}(r)\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Q\left((C-C_{n}^{*})_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*}),(C-C_{n}^{*})_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\right).

Now, using Proposition 4 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
, we get for some
\zeta>0
that

\displaystyle T\mathbb{E}(Z_{C}(N_{n}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}Q\left((C-C_{n}^{*})_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}g,(C-C_{n}^{*})_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}g\right)

\displaystyle\geq\zeta\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|(C-C_{n}^{*})_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{2}^{2}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\geq\zeta\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{2}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|C_{iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-C_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}\|_{2}^{2}.

Thus, in this special case, we can prove that

\phi_{\text{comp}}(S_{1}(C_{n}^{*}),...,S_{n}(C_{n}^{*});L;\mathcal{H}_{n})\geq\frac{\zeta}{2}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}*)\|_{2}^{2}>0

with probability converging to one.

Remark E.1
.

The discussion above can be transferred directly to individual compatibility constant defined in Section
3.1
. First note that, in the special case discussed here, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\left\|\Psi_{n,i}(\cdot;C_{i},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})-\Psi_{n,i}(\cdot;C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{*});\alpha_{n,i}^{*}(\theta_{n})^{*})\right\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\left\|\Psi_{n,i}(\cdot;C_{i},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})-\lambda_{n,i}(\cdot)\right\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(C_{ij}-C_{n,ij}^{*}\right)\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{j}(r)\right)^{2}dt=\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}(Z_{i,C}\circ\mathcal{S}_{t})(N_{n})dt,

where

Z_{C,i}(N_{n}):=\frac{1}{T}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(C_{ij}-C_{n,ij}^{*}\right)\int_{-A}^{0-}g(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{j}(r)\right)^{2}.

Hence, we may repeat the above arguments by replacing
Z_{C}(N_{n})
by
Z_{C,i}(N_{n})
to obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\left\|\Psi_{n,i}(\cdot;C_{i},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})-\Psi_{n,i}(\cdot;C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{*});\alpha_{n,i}^{*}(\theta_{n})^{*})\right\|_{T}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}T\mathbb{E}(Z_{i,C}(N_{n}))

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}Q((C-C_{n}^{*})_{i\cdot}g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*}),(C-C_{n}^{*})_{i\cdot}g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*}))\geq\frac{\zeta}{2}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{2}^{2}

simultaneously for all
i=1,...,n
with probability converging to one. This implies that

\mathbb{P}(\cap_{i=1}^{n}\Omega_{\text{IRCC}}(L,\phi_{0};B_{r}(\theta_{n}*)))\to 1

with
\phi_{0}:=\sqrt{\frac{\zeta}{2}}\|g(\cdot;\gamma_{n}^{*})\|_{2}
.

Appendix F
Proofs of Section
B.2

Proof of Lemma
B.3
.

The proof takes many ideas from the proofs of Lemma 1 and Proposition 2 in
Hansen et al. (2015)
. However, for our purposes, we need to refine some of the arguments in order to prove a stronger bound on the sum of the expectations. To this end, we need to keep track of the constants and use the sparsity assumption in order to obtain a uniform result which holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In the interest of completeness we give the full proof here. Denote for all
i,l=1,...,n
by

K_{i}^{l}(0):=W_{i}^{l}(0)\textrm{ and }K_{i}^{l}(k):=W_{i}^{l}(k)-W_{i}^{l}(k-1)\textrm{ for }k\geq 1

the number of events in the
k
-th generation in a cluster which has started from an initial event in the
l
-th process. Let
K^{l}(k):=(K_{1}^{l}(k),...,K_{n}^{l}(k))
and define the logarithm of the Laplace transform of
K^{l}(1)
by

\phi_{n,l}(s):=\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}K^{l}(1)}\right).

Let finally
\phi_{n}:=(\phi_{n,1},...,\phi_{n,n})
. Let
a:=\int_{0}^{\infty}g(t;\gamma)dt
. We note that
K^{l}_{j}(1)
for
j=1,...,n
equals the number of events of a counting process with intensity function
C_{n,jl}g(t;\gamma)
and hence
K^{l}_{j}(1)
is Poisson distributed with rate
C_{n,jl}
. Moreover, for any
l
, the variables
K_{1}^{l}(1),...,K_{n}^{l}(1)
are independent. It is therefore direct to compute that

\phi_{n,l}(s)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{n,jl}\left(e^{s_{j}}-1\right).

(48)

Then, we obtain the following estimate for all
s\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
\|s\|_{1}\leq r
(recall the definition of
r
and
\varepsilon
, and note that in particular
|s_{j}|\leq r
for all
j
)

\|\phi_{n}(s)\|_{1}\leq\sum_{l,j=1}^{n}aC_{n,jl}\left|e^{s_{j}}-1\right|\leq a_{0}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left|e^{s_{j}}-1\right|\leq\varepsilon\|s\|_{1}.

(49)

Thus, all
\phi_{n}
are contractions with the same constant
\varepsilon
. Moreover, we prove next that for any
p\geq 1
and all
s\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}K^{l}(p)}\Big|K^{l}(p-1),...,K^{l}(0)\right)=e^{\phi_{n}(s)^{T}K^{l}(p-1)}.

(50)

Proof of (
50
).

We bring the following intuition in formulas:
The distribution of the number of new events in the
p
-th generation of the
i
-th process can be obtained by starting for each event in any of the processes
j=1,...,n
in the
(p-1)
-th generation a new counting process with intensity
C_{n,ij}g(t;\gamma)
and summing all their event numbers (which are iid copies of
K_{i}^{j}(1)
)
. To write this in formulas, let
K_{i,r}^{j}(1)
be independent copies of
K_{i}^{j}(1)
. Then, the following equality holds in distribution

K_{i}^{l}(p)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{r=1}^{K_{j}^{l}(p-1)}K_{i,r}^{j}(1).

From this we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}K^{l}(p)}\Big|K^{l}(p-1),...,K^{l}(0)\right)=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\prod_{r=1}^{K_{j}^{l}(p-1)}\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}K_{i,r}^{j}(1)}\Big|K^{l}(p-1),...,K^{l}(0)\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{j=1}^{n}\prod_{r=1}^{K_{j}^{l}(p-1)}e^{\phi_{n,j}(s)}=\prod_{j=1}^{n}e^{\phi_{n,j}(s)K_{j}^{l}(p-1)}=e^{\phi_{n}(s)^{T}K^{l}(p-1)}.

∎

We consider now the following recursively defined functions
g^{(p)}:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}

g^{(0)}(s):=s\textrm{ and }g^{(p)}(s)=s+\phi_{n}\left(g^{(p-1)}(s)\right),\textrm{ for }p\geq 1.

In the next step we prove the following relation for all
k,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
k\geq 0
,
m\geq 1
, and
k+m\geq 2
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(e^{-s^{T}(W^{l}(k)-W^{l}(k+m-2))+g^{(1)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k+m-1)}\Big|K^{l}(k),...,K^{l}(0)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{g^{(m-1)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k+1)}\Big|K^{l}(k),...,K^{l}(0)\right).

(51)

Proof of (
51
).

We show (
51
) via induction over
m\geq 1
. For
m=1
we have by the definitions of
K^{l}(k)
,
g^{(p)}
and by (
50
) for all
k\geq 1

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}K^{l}(k+1)}\Big|K^{l}(k),...,K^{l}(0)\right)=e^{\phi_{n}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k)}=e^{\left(-s+g^{(1)}(s)\right)^{T}K^{l}(k)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{-s^{T}(W^{l}(k)-W^{l}(k-1))+g^{(1)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k)}\Big|K^{l}(k),....,K^{l}(0)\right).

The case
k=0
and
m=2
is trivial. Thus, the induction start is complete. In the induction step, we suppose
k\geq 1
first. Suppose now that (
51
) holds for some
m-1
, i.e.
m\geq 2
. We have then by definition of
g^{(p)}
, (
50
), the induction hypothesis and definition of
K^{l}(k)
for all
k\geq 1

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(e^{g^{(m-1)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k+1)}\Big|K^{l}(k),...,K^{l}(0)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}K^{l}(k+1)}e^{\phi_{n}(g^{(m-2)}(s))^{T}K^{l}(k+1)}\Big|K^{l}(k),...,K^{l}(0)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}K^{l}(k+1)}\mathbb{E}\left(e^{g^{(m-2)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k+2)}\Big|K^{l}(k+1),...,K^{l}(0)\right)\Big|K^{l}(k),...,K^{l}(0)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg(e^{s^{T}K^{l}(k+1)}\mathbb{E}\left(e^{-s^{T}(W^{l}(k+1)-W^{l}(k+m-2))+g^{(1)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k+m-1)}\Big|K^{l}(k+1),...,K^{l}(0)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\Big|K^{l}(k),...,K^{l}(0)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{-s^{T}(W^{l}(k+1)-K^{l}(k+1)-W^{l}(k+m-2))+g^{(1)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k+m-1)}\Big|K^{l}(k),...,K^{l}(0)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{-s^{T}(W^{l}(k)-W^{l}(k+m-2))+g^{(1)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k+m-1)}\Big|K^{l}(k),...,K^{l}(0)\right)

and the induction is complete. It remains to consider the case
k=0
which can be proven by similar arguments.
∎

By the tower rule, (
51
) holds also without the conditions in the expectations. Let now either
k=0
and
p\geq 2
or
p>k\geq 1
for
p,k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then, we can apply (
51
) with
m=p-k
to obtain (use first the definition ok
K^{l}(p)
, in the second equality (
50
), and in the third equality (
51
))

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}(W^{l}(p)-W^{l}(k))}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{-s^{T}W^{l}(k)+s^{T}W^{l}(p-1)}\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}K^{l}(p)}\Big|K^{l}(p-1),...,K^{l}(0)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{-s^{T}(W^{l}(k)-W^{l}(p-2))+g^{(1)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(p-1)}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{g^{(p-k-1)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k+1)}\right).

By applying (
50
) repeatedly, we can continue

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(e^{g^{(p-k-1)}(s)^{T}K^{l}(k+1)}\Big|K^{l}(k),...,K^{l}(1)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\phi_{n}(g^{(p-k-1)}(s))^{T}K^{l}(k)}\right)=...=\mathbb{E}\left(e^{\phi_{n}^{\circ(k+1)}(g^{(p-k-1)}(s))^{T}K^{l}(0)}\right)=e^{\left[\phi_{n}^{\circ(k+1)}(g^{(p-k-1)}(s))\right]_{l}},

where
\phi_{n}^{\circ(k)}:=\phi_{n}\circ\dots\circ\phi_{n}

k
-times. By manually checking the case
p=1
and
k=0
we conclude that the following equality holds for all
p>k\geq 0

\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}\left(W^{l}(p)-W^{l}(k)\right)}\right)=e^{\left[\phi_{n}^{\circ(k+1)}\left(g^{(p-k-1)}(s)\right)\right]_{l}}.

(52)

In particular, we obtain for
k=0

\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}W^{l}(p)}\right)=s_{l}+\phi_{n}\left(g^{(p-1)}(s)\right)_{l}=g^{(p)}(s)_{l}.

By using (
49
) it can be shown that
\|g^{(p)}(s)\|_{1}\leq r
for all
p\in\mathbb{N}
if
\|s\|_{1}\leq r(1-\varepsilon)
. Note that for each fixed
i
the sequence
W_{i}^{l}(K)
is increasing as
K
grows. Hence,
W_{i}^{l}=\lim_{K\to\infty}W_{i}^{l}(K)\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\cup\{\infty\}
exists. Let
s\in[0,\infty)^{n}
be arbitrary. By the monotone convergence theorem we conclude that

\displaystyle x_{l}(s)

\displaystyle:=\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}W^{l}}\right)=\log\mathbb{E}\left(\lim_{K\to\infty}e^{s^{T}W^{l}(K)}\right)=\lim_{K\to\infty}\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}W^{l}(K)}\right)=\lim_{K\to\infty}g^{(K)}(s)_{l}.

(53)

Thus, we get for
\|s\|_{1}\leq r(1-\varepsilon)
,

\sum_{l=1}^{n}\left|\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}W^{l}}\right)\right|=\|x(s)\|_{1}=\lim_{K\to\infty}\left\|g^{(K)}(s)\right\|_{1}\leq r.

Thus, we have proven (
21
).

In order to prove (
22
), we firstly note (
21
) implies for
\|s\|_{1}\leq r(1+\varepsilon)
with
s\in[0,\infty)^{n}

\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}(W^{l}-W^{l}(k))}\right)\leq\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}W^{l}}\right)\leq r

because
s\in[0,\infty)
and monotonicity of
W_{i}^{l}(k)
in
k
imply that the expectations are larger or equal than one and hence, the logarithms are non-negative. Thus, by definition of
\varepsilon,r

\left|\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}(W^{l}-W^{l}(k))}\right)-1\right|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{a_{0}}\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}(W^{l}-W^{l}(k))}\right).

We therefore prove in the following that

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}(W^{l}-W^{l}(k))}\right)\leq\frac{r\varepsilon}{1-\varepsilon}.

We use monotone convergence, (
52
), and continuity of
\phi_{n}
to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n}\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}(W^{l}-W^{l}(k))}\right)=\sum_{l=1}^{n}\lim_{p\to\infty}\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}(W^{l}(p)-W^{l}(k))}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{n}\left[\phi_{n}^{\circ(k+1)}\left(\lim_{p\to\infty}g^{(p-k-1)}(s)\right)\right]_{l}=\sum_{l=1}^{n}\left[\phi_{n}^{\circ(k+1)}(x(s))\right]_{l}\leq\left\|\phi_{n}^{\circ(k+1)}(x(s))\right\|_{1}.

Note that
\|\phi^{\circ k}_{n}(x(s))\|_{1}\leq\varepsilon^{k}r
by (
49
) and since
\|x(s)\|_{1}\leq r
as we have just proven in (
21
). Hence,

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l=1}^{n}\log\mathbb{E}\left(e^{s^{T}(W^{l}-W^{l}(k))}\right)\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{k+1}r=\frac{\varepsilon r}{1-\varepsilon}.

Thus, the proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

Appendix G
Proofs of Section
3.1

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

The proof runs along the same lines of the proof of Theorem 6.2 in
Bühlmann and van de Geer (2011)
. However, for completeness we repeat it here in our setting. Define

\displaystyle\widecheck{P}_{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\textrm{ and }P^{*}_{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*}\|_{1}.

With this, we obtain by the definition of
(\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nT}\mathcal{E}(\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})+2\widecheck{P}_{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\textrm{LS}_{i}(\widecheck{C}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n,i},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})+2\int_{0}^{T}\Psi_{n,i}(t;\widecheck{C}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n,i},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})dM_{n,i}(t)\right)+2\widecheck{P}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\textrm{LS}_{i}(C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})+2\int_{0}^{T}\Psi_{n,i}(t;\widecheck{C}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n,i},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})dM_{n,i}(t)\right)+2P_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nT}\mathcal{E}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})+2P_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg(\frac{2}{nT}\int_{0}^{T}\Psi_{n,i}(t;\widecheck{C}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n,i},\widecheck{\theta}_{n,i})dM_{n,i}(t)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{2}{nT}\int_{0}^{T}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\Bigg)

(54)

On the event
\mathcal{T}_{n}(a_{n},b_{n},d_{n},e_{n})
, defined in (
10
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\Psi_{n,i}(t;\widecheck{C}_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n,i},\widecheck{\theta}_{n,i})dM_{n,i}(t)-\int_{0}^{T}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\widecheck{\alpha}_{n,i}-\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\right)\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\widecheck{\beta}_{n})dM_{n,i}(t)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\left(\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\widecheck{\beta}_{n})-\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{n}^{*})\right)dM_{n,i}(t)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\widecheck{\gamma}_{n})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\cdot\left(\widecheck{C}_{n,ij}-C_{n,ij}^{*}\right)\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}\left(g(t-r;\widecheck{\gamma}_{n})-g(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})\right)dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\cdot C_{n,ij}^{*}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{T}\sup_{i=1,...,n}\sup_{\overline{\beta}\in K_{\beta}}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\overline{\beta})dM_{n,i}(t)\right|\cdot\frac{1}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\widecheck{\beta}_{n})-\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{n}^{*})}{\left\|\widecheck{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}}dM_{n,i}(t)\right|\cdot\left\|\widecheck{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sup_{j=1,...,n}\left|\frac{2}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\widecheck{\gamma}_{n})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\right|\cdot\left\|\widecheck{C}_{n,i\cdot}-C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\right\|_{1}

\displaystyle+\left|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}\frac{g(t-r;\widecheck{\gamma}_{n})-g(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})}{\left|\widecheck{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{n}^{*}\right|}dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\cdot C_{n,ij}^{*}\right|\cdot\left|\widecheck{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{n}^{*}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+b_{n}\left\|\widecheck{\beta}_{n}-\beta_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{n,i}\left\|\widecheck{C}_{n,i\cdot}-C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\right\|_{1}+e_{n}|\widecheck{\gamma}_{n}-\gamma_{n}^{*}|

\displaystyle=\frac{a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{n,i}\left\|\widecheck{C}_{n,i\cdot}-C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1},

where
\widetilde{b}_{n}\geq\max(b_{n},e_{n})
. Using this in (
54
) yields on the event
\mathcal{T}_{n}(a_{n},b_{n},d_{n},e_{n})
the following
basic inequality:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nT}\mathcal{E}(\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})+2\widecheck{P}_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{nT}\mathcal{E}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})+2P_{n}^{*}

\displaystyle+\frac{a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{n,i}\left\|\widecheck{C}_{n,i\cdot}-C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1}.

(55)

Denote for any
S\subseteq\{1,...,n\}

\displaystyle P_{n,i,S}^{*}:

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega_{i}}{n}\|C_{n,iS}^{*}\|_{1},\quad\widecheck{P}_{n,i,S}:=\frac{\omega_{i}}{n}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS}\|_{1}.

Note that
P_{n,i,S_{i}^{c}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}=0
. Then, we obtain (use (
55
) in the second inequality, the reverse triangle inequality in the last line, and
d_{n,i}\leq\omega_{i}
several times):

\displaystyle\frac{2}{nT}\mathcal{E}(\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})+2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widecheck{P}_{n,i,S_{i}^{c}(C_{n}^{*})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{nT}\mathcal{E}(\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})+4\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widecheck{P}_{n,i,S_{i}^{c}(C_{n}^{*})}-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{n,i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}^{c}(C_{n}^{*})}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{nT}\mathcal{E}(\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})+4\widecheck{P}_{n}-4\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widecheck{P}_{n,i,S_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{n,i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}^{c}(C_{n}^{*})}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{nT}\mathcal{E}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})+4P_{n}^{*}+\frac{2a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{n,i}\left\|\widecheck{C}_{n,i\cdot}-C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\right\|_{1}+2\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle-4\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widecheck{P}_{n,i,S_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{n,i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}^{c}(C_{n}^{*})}\|_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{nT}\mathcal{E}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})+4P_{n}^{*}-4\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widecheck{P}_{n,i,S_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}

\displaystyle+\frac{2a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}d_{n,i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-C_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}\|_{1}+2\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{nT}\mathcal{E}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})+4\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{n,i,S_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}-4\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widecheck{P}_{n,i,S_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}

\displaystyle+\frac{2a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-C_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}\|_{1}+2\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{nT}\mathcal{E}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})

\displaystyle+\frac{2a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{6}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-C_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}\|_{1}+2\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1}

By the definition of
\mathcal{E}
and since
\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})=\lambda_{n,i}(t)
this is equivalent to

\displaystyle\frac{2}{nT}\left\|\Psi_{n}(\cdot;\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})-\lambda_{n}\right\|_{T}^{2}+2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widecheck{P}_{n,i,S_{i}^{c}(C_{n}^{*})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{6}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-C_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}\|_{1}+2\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1}.

(56)

Replacing the definition of
\widecheck{P}_{n,i,S_{i}^{c}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*})}
, the above implies in particular that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}^{c}(C_{n}^{*})}-C_{n,iS_{i}^{c}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{3}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-C_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}\|_{1}+\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1}.

Since we are on
\Omega_{RCC,n}(L,H_{n})
and
L\geq\max(3\omega_{1},...,3\omega_{n},a_{n},\widetilde{b}_{n})/\min(\omega_{1},...,\omega_{n})
, we can apply the assertion of the random compatibility condition. More precisely, this means by using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{3a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{8}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-C_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}\|_{1}+3\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{L}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*})\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-C_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}\|^{2}+\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{L}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*})}{\phi_{\textrm{comp}}(S_{1}(C_{n}^{*}),...,S_{n}(C_{n}^{*});L;\mathcal{H}_{n})}\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\|\Psi_{n}(\cdot;\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})-\lambda_{n}\|_{T}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{L}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*})^{2}}{4\phi_{\textrm{comp}}(S_{1}(C_{n}^{*}),...,S_{n}(C_{n}^{*});L;\mathcal{H}_{n})^{2}}+\frac{1}{nT}\|\Psi_{n}(\cdot;\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})-\lambda_{n}\|_{T}^{2}.

(57)

Thus, we obtain from (
56
)

\displaystyle\frac{2}{nT}\left\|\Psi_{n}(\cdot;\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})-\lambda_{n}\right\|_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,i\cdot}-C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\|_{1}+\frac{a_{n}}{n}\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\|_{1}+\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3a_{n}}{n}\left\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\right\|_{1}+\frac{8}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}-C_{n,iS_{i}(C_{n}^{*})}^{*}\|_{1}+3\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{L}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*})^{2}}{4\phi_{\textrm{comp}}(S_{1}(C_{n}^{*}),...,S_{n}(C_{n}^{*});L;\mathcal{H}_{n})^{2}}+\frac{1}{nT}\|\Psi_{n}(\cdot;\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})-\lambda_{n}\|_{T}^{2}.

which in turn implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nT}\left\|\Psi_{n}(\cdot;\widecheck{C}_{n},\widecheck{\alpha}_{n},\widecheck{\theta}_{n})-\lambda_{n}\right\|_{T}^{2}+\frac{2}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\omega_{i}\|\widecheck{C}_{n,i\cdot}-C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\|_{1}+\frac{a_{n}}{n}\|\widecheck{\alpha}_{n}-\alpha_{n}^{*}\|_{1}+\widetilde{b}_{n}\|\widecheck{\theta}_{n}-\theta_{n}^{*}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{L}(C_{n}^{*},\alpha_{n}^{*})^{2}}{4\phi_{\textrm{comp}}(S_{1}(C_{n}^{*}),...,S_{n}(C_{n}^{*});L;\mathcal{H}_{n})^{2}}.

∎

G.1
Further details to the proof of Lemma
C.1

Part involving
b_{n}
:

We firstly note that by differentiability of
\nu_{0}

\displaystyle\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\overline{\beta})-\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{n}^{*})}{\big\|\overline{\beta}-\beta_{n}^{*}\big\|_{1}}dM_{n,i}(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\big(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\overline{\beta}\big)^{T}\frac{\big(\overline{\beta}-\beta_{n}^{*}\big)}{\big\|\overline{\beta}-\beta_{n}^{*}\big\|_{1}}dsdM_{n,i}(t)

Note that, by convexity,
(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\overline{\beta}\in K_{\beta}
for all
s\in[0,T]
. We can use a standard
chaining light
argument as follows: Let
K_{\beta,n,\eta}
be as in the part involving
a_{n}
. Let similarly
L^{1}_{\eta}\subseteq\{\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\|\beta\|_{1}=1\}
be a finite set such that for each
\beta\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
with
\|\beta\|_{1}=1
there is
Q_{n}(\beta)\in L^{1}_{\eta}
such that
\|\beta-Q_{n}(\beta)\|\leq\eta
. By adjusting
K_{0}>0
, it is possible to choose
K_{\beta,n,\eta}
and
L^{1}_{\eta}
such that
|K_{\beta,n,\eta}|,|L^{1}_{\eta}|\leq K_{0}\eta^{-p}
. As before, we define
\eta
at this point without motivation. Choose first
c_{2}^{\prime\prime}
such that

\displaystyle 2K_{\alpha}\big(\frac{\mathcal{N}(T+A)}{AT}+K_{\alpha}\|\overline{\nu}\|_{\infty}+c_{n}\overline{g}\mathcal{N}\big)\big(\frac{D_{\nu}}{2}+L_{\nu}\big)\leq c_{2}^{\prime\prime}c_{n}\log(nT).

Let
\eta:=\frac{4K_{\alpha}L_{\nu}(2p+\alpha_{2})}{c_{2}^{\prime\prime}\sqrt{\mu-\phi(\mu)}c_{n}nT}
. We conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{\overline{\beta}\in K_{\beta}}\Big|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\overline{\beta})-\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{n}^{*})}{\big\|\overline{\beta}-\beta_{n}^{*}\big\|_{1}}dM_{n,i}(t)\Big|>b_{n}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{\overline{\beta}\in K_{\beta}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{2\alpha_{n,i}^{*}}{nT}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\big(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\overline{\beta}\big)^{T}\frac{\big(\overline{\beta}-\beta_{n}^{*}\big)}{\big\|\overline{\beta}-\beta_{n}^{*}\big\|_{1}}dsdM_{n,i}(t)\Big|>b_{n}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\underset{\beta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\|\beta_{2}\|_{1}=1}{\sup_{\beta_{1}\in K_{\beta},}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{2\alpha_{n,i}}{nT}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\big(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\big)^{T}\beta_{2}dsdM_{n,i}(t)\Big|>b_{n}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\scalebox{0.95}{$\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\underset{\beta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\|\beta_{2}\|_{1}=1}{\sup_{\beta_{1}\in K_{\beta},}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{2\alpha_{n,i}}{nT}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\big(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\big)^{T}\beta_{2}dsdM_{n,i}(t)\Big|>b_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 284.52756pt+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\underset{\beta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\|\beta_{2}\|_{1}=1}{\sup_{\beta_{1}\in K_{\beta},}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{2\alpha_{n,i}}{nT}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\big(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\big)^{T}\beta_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\big(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+sP_{n}(\beta_{1})\big)^{T}Q_{n}(\beta_{2})dsdM_{n,i}(t)\Big|>\frac{b_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

(58)

\displaystyle+\scalebox{0.95}{$\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\underset{\beta_{2}\in L^{1}_{\eta}}{\sup_{\beta_{1}\in K_{\beta,n,\eta},}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{2\alpha_{n,i}}{nT}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\big(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\big)^{T}\beta_{2}dsdM_{n,i}(t)\Big|>\frac{b_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 284.52756pt+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}^{c})

(59)

We begin with the probability in (
59
). By a union bound argument, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\underset{\beta_{2}\in L^{1}_{\eta}}{\sup_{\beta_{1}\in K_{\beta,n,\eta},}}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{2\alpha_{n,i}}{nT}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\right)^{T}\beta_{2}dsdM_{n,i}(t)\Big|>\frac{b_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\scalebox{0.89}{$\displaystyle{K_{0}^{2}\eta^{-2p}\sup_{\beta_{1}\in K_{\beta,n,\eta}\atop\beta_{2}\in L^{1}_{\eta}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\right)^{T}\beta_{2}dsdM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\frac{b_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right).}$}

(60)

The above probability can be handled by Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
. We use again the same notation from the original paper for ease of the reader. We apply this time the multivariate version with
M=n
and

H_{i}(t)=\frac{4\alpha_{n,i}^{*}}{nT}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\right)^{T}\beta_{2}ds.

We check now the conditions of the previously mentioned theorem.
|H_{i}(t)|\leq\frac{4K_{\alpha}}{nT}L_{\nu}=:B
and the integral conditions are fulfilled. We consider the constant stopping time
\tau=T
. Define next

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{b}^{\mu}:

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu}{\mu-\phi(\mu)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{16(\alpha_{n,i}^{*})^{2}}{n^{2}T^{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\right)^{T}\beta_{2}ds\right)^{2}dN_{n,i}(t)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{16K_{\alpha}^{2}L_{\nu}^{2}x}{n^{2}T^{2}(\mu-\phi(\mu))}.

On
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
, we have

w:=\frac{16K_{\alpha}^{2}L_{\nu}^{2}x}{n^{2}T^{2}(\mu-\phi(\mu))}\leq\widehat{V}_{b}^{\mu}\leq\frac{16\mu K_{\alpha}^{2}L_{\nu}^{2}\mathcal{N}(T+A)}{nT^{2}A(\mu-\phi(\mu))}+\frac{16K_{\alpha}^{2}L_{\nu}^{2}x}{n^{2}T^{2}(\mu-\phi(\mu))}=:v.

Hence, application of Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
shows for
\varepsilon=1
and
x=(2p+\alpha_{2})\log(Tn)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\Big[\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\right)^{T}\beta_{2}\Big]dsdM_{n,i}(t)>\frac{b_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}H_{i}(t)dM_{n,i}(t)>2\sqrt{\widehat{V}_{b}^{\mu}x}+\frac{Bx}{3},w\leq\widehat{V}_{b}^{\mu}\leq v,\sup_{i,t}|H_{i}(t)|\leq B\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu\mathcal{N}n(T+A)}{Ax}+1\right)+1\right)e^{-x}\leq c_{2}^{\prime}\log(nT)(nT)^{-(2p+\alpha_{2})}

for a suitable constant
c_{2}^{\prime}>0
. Combining the above with (
60
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\underset{\beta_{2}\in L^{1}_{\eta}}{\sup_{\beta_{1}\in K_{\beta,n,\eta},}}\Bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{2\alpha_{n,i}^{*}}{nT}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\right)^{T}\beta_{2}dsdM_{n,i}(t)\Bigg|>\frac{b_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2K_{0}^{2}\eta^{-2p}c_{2}^{\prime}\log(nT)(nT)^{-(2p+\alpha_{2})}=c_{2}\frac{c_{n}^{2p}\log(nT)}{(nT)^{\alpha_{2}}}

for a suitable choice of
c_{2}
.

We turn now to (
58
). For ease of notation, we denote below
d|M_{n,i}(t)|:=dN_{n,i}(t)+\lambda_{n,i}(t)dt
. We note that, by Lipschitz continuity of
\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}
, on
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}

\displaystyle\underset{\beta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\|\beta_{2}\|_{1}=1}{\sup_{\beta_{1}\in K_{\beta},}}\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\right)^{T}\beta_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+sP(\beta_{1})\right)^{T}Q_{n}(\beta_{2})dsdM_{n,i}(t)\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\underset{\beta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\|\beta_{2}\|_{1}=1}{\sup_{\beta_{1}\in K_{\beta},}}\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\Big(\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+sP(\beta_{1})\right)\Big)^{T}\beta_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+sP(\beta_{1})\right)^{T}(Q_{n}(\beta_{2})-\beta_{2})dsdM_{n,i}(t)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\underset{\beta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}:\|\beta_{2}\|_{1}=1}{\sup_{\beta_{1}\in K_{\beta},}}\frac{2K_{\alpha}}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{1}\Big\|\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+s\beta_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+sP(\beta_{1})\right)\Big\|_{\infty}\|\beta_{2}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\Big\|\frac{d}{d\beta}\nu_{0}\left(X_{n,i}(t);(1-s)\beta_{n}^{*}+sP(\beta_{1})\right)\Big\|\|Q_{n}(\beta_{2})-\beta_{2}\|dsd|M_{n,i}|(t)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2K_{\alpha}}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{D_{\nu}}{2}\eta+L_{\nu}\eta d|M_{n,i}|(t)

\displaystyle=\frac{2K_{\alpha}}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{D_{\nu}}{2}\eta+L_{\nu}\eta dN_{n,i}(t)+\frac{2K_{\alpha}}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\frac{D_{\nu}}{2}\eta+L_{\nu}\eta\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\Big(\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{n}^{*})+\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{n,j}(r)\Big)dt

\displaystyle\leq 2K_{\alpha}\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}(T+A)}{AT}+K_{\alpha}\|\overline{\nu}\|_{\infty}+\sup_{i=1,..,n}\|C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\|_{1}\overline{g}\mathcal{N}\right)\left(\frac{D_{\nu}}{2}+L_{\nu}\right)\eta

\displaystyle\leq 2K_{\alpha}\left(\frac{\mathcal{N}(T+A)}{AT}+K_{\alpha}\|\overline{\nu}\|_{\infty}+c_{n}\overline{g}\mathcal{N}\right)\left(\frac{D_{\nu}}{2}+L_{\nu}\right)\eta\leq c_{2}^{\prime\prime}c_{n}\log(nT)\eta,

by choice of
c_{2}^{\prime\prime}
, (
28
), and the fact that the number of jumps of each
N_{n,i}
on
[0,T]
is on
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
bounded by
\mathcal{N}(T+A)/A
. By choice of
\eta
, we have that

c_{2}^{\prime\prime}c_{n}\log(nT)\eta\leq\sqrt{wx}\leq\sqrt{\widehat{V}_{b}^{\mu}x}\leq\frac{b_{n}}{2}.

Hence,
\eqref{eq:bn_cont}=0
.

Part involving
d_{n,i}
:
Let
K_{\gamma,n,\eta}
be a finite, discrete grid covering
K_{\gamma}
such that for each
\gamma\in K_{\gamma}
, there is
P_{n}(\gamma)\in K_{\gamma,n,\eta}
such that
|\gamma-P_{n}(\gamma)|\leq\eta
. It is possible to choose
K_{\gamma,n,\eta}
such that
|K_{\gamma,n,\eta}|\leq K_{1}\eta^{-1}
. Let
c_{3,i}^{\prime\prime}>0
be such that

2L_{g}\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{(T+A)\mathcal{N}}{AT}+K_{\alpha}\overline{\nu}_{i}+c_{n}\overline{g}\mathcal{N}\right)\leq c_{3,i}^{\prime\prime}\log(nT)^{2}c_{n}

and

\eta_{i}:=\frac{24\overline{g}\mathcal{N}_{0}\left(\log n+\log(nT)+\alpha_{3}\log T\right)}{\sqrt{\mu-\phi(\mu)}c_{3,i}^{\prime\prime}\log(nT)c_{n}T}.

We now have by the standard union bound and a
chaining light
argument

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\exists i\in\{1,...,n\}:\sup_{j=1,...,n}\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma}}\frac{2}{T}\Bigg|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\Bigg|>d_{n,i}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{2}\sup_{i,j=1,...,n}\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma}}\frac{2}{T}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>d_{n,i},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Big)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\underset{i,j=1,...,n}{n^{2}\sup}\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma}}\frac{2}{T}\Big|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})-g(t-r;P_{n}(\overline{\gamma}))dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\Big|>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

(61)

\displaystyle+\underset{i,j=1,...,n}{n^{2}\sup}\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma,n,\eta_{i}}}\frac{2}{T}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}^{c})

(62)

For (
62
), we can continue using union bound

\displaystyle n^{2}\sup_{i,j=1,...,n}\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma,n,\eta_{i}}}\frac{2}{T}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{2}K_{1}\eta_{i}^{-1}\sup_{i,j=1,...,n}\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma,n,\eta_{i}}}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{2}{T}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right).

(63)

We can apply now again Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
in its univariate form, that is, with
M=1
. Let

H(t):=\frac{4}{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)\mathbbm{1}(t\leq\tau_{n}).

We have
|H(t)|\leq\frac{4\overline{g}\mathcal{N}}{T}=:B
which implies the integral conditions (see also the proof of Theorem 2 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
). Define
x:=\log n+\log(nT)+\alpha_{3}\log T
and

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{d}^{\mu}

\displaystyle:=\frac{16\mu}{T^{2}(\mu-\phi(\mu))}\int_{0}^{\tau_{n}}\left(\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)\right)^{2}dN_{n,i}(t)+\frac{16\overline{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}x}{T^{2}(\mu-\phi(\mu))}

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{d,0}^{\mu}

\displaystyle:=\frac{16\mu}{T^{2}(\mu-\phi(\mu))}\int_{0}^{T}\left(\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)\right)^{2}dN_{n,i}(t)+\frac{16\overline{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}x}{T^{2}(\mu-\phi(\mu))}.

We bound on
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
, using (
28
),

w:=\frac{16\overline{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}x}{T^{2}(\mu-\phi(\mu))}\leq\widehat{V}_{d}^{\mu}\leq\frac{16\mu}{T^{2}(\mu-\phi(\mu))}\frac{T+A}{A}\overline{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{3}+\frac{16\overline{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}x}{T^{2}(\mu-\phi(\mu))}=:v.

Using Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
we hence have for
\varepsilon=1

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{2}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{4}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)>2\sqrt{\widehat{V}_{d,0}^{\mu}x}+\frac{Bx}{3},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{4}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)>2\sqrt{\widehat{V}_{d}^{\mu}x}+\frac{Bx}{3},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\frac{4}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)>2\sqrt{\widehat{V}_{d}^{\mu}x}+\frac{Bx}{3},w\leq\widehat{V}_{d}^{\mu}\leq v,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\sup_{t\leq\tau_{n}}|H(t)|\leq B\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu(T+A)\mathcal{N}}{Ax}+1\right)+1\right)e^{-x}=c_{3}^{\prime}\log T\cdot n^{-2}T^{-(1+\alpha_{3})}

for a suitable constant
c_{3}^{\prime}
. Using the above in (
63
) yields by the definition of
\eta_{i}
.

\displaystyle n^{2}\sup_{i,j=1,...,n}\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma,n,\eta_{i}}}\frac{2}{T}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq n^{2}K_{1}\max_{i=1,...,n}\frac{\sqrt{\mu-\phi(\mu)}c_{3,i}^{\prime\prime}\log(nT)c_{n}T}{24\overline{g}\mathcal{N}_{0}\left(\log n+\log(nT)+\alpha_{3}\log T\right)}2c_{3}^{\prime}\log T\cdot n^{-2}T^{-(1+\alpha_{3})}

\displaystyle\leq c_{3}\frac{c_{n}\log T}{T^{\alpha_{3}}}

for a suitable
c_{3}>0
.

To bound (
61
), we use Lipschitz continuity of
\gamma
. Denote again
d|M_{n,i}|(t):=dN_{n,i}(t)+\lambda_{n,i}(t)dt
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma}}\frac{2}{T}\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})-g(t-r;P_{n}(\overline{\gamma}))dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{2}{T}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{t-A}^{t-}L_{g}\eta_{i}dN_{n,j}(r)d|M_{n,i}|(t)>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{2}{T}\int_{0}^{T}L_{g}\eta_{i}\mathcal{N}d|M_{n,i}|(t)>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big(\frac{2}{T}\int_{0}^{T}L_{g}\eta_{i}\mathcal{N}dN_{n,i}(t)+\frac{2}{T}\int_{0}^{T}L_{g}\eta_{i}\mathcal{N}\Big(\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{n}^{*})\,+

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{j}(r)\Big)dt>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\frac{2L_{g}\eta_{i}(T+A)\mathcal{N}^{2}}{AT}+2L_{g}\eta_{i}\mathcal{N}\left(K_{\alpha}\overline{\nu}_{i}+\|C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\overline{g}\mathcal{N}\right)>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(2L_{g}\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{(T+A)\mathcal{N}}{AT}+K_{\alpha}\overline{\nu}_{i}+c_{n}\overline{g}\mathcal{N}\right)\eta_{i}>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(c_{3,i}^{\prime\prime}\log^{2}(nT)c_{n}\eta_{i}>\frac{d_{n,i}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right).

By definition of
\eta_{i}
, we have, however
c_{3,i}^{\prime\prime}\log^{2}(nT)c_{n}\eta_{i}\leq\sqrt{wx}\leq d_{n,i}/2
on
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
and hence the above probability equals
0
.

Part involving
e_{n}
:
Let
K_{\gamma,\eta,n}
be the same grid as in the part involving
d_{n,i}
. Let
c_{4}^{\prime\prime}>0
be chosen such that

\displaystyle\frac{D_{g}\mathcal{N}c_{n}}{T}\left(\frac{T+A}{A}\mathcal{N}+TK_{\alpha}\|\overline{\nu}\|_{\infty}+c_{n}\overline{g}\mathcal{N}T\right)\leq c_{4}^{\prime\prime}c_{n}\left(1+c_{n}\right)\log^{2}(nT).

We define then

\eta:=\frac{24L_{g}\mathcal{N}_{0}(1+\alpha_{4})}{\sqrt{\mu-\phi(\mu)}c_{4}^{\prime\prime}(1+c_{n})nT}.

We compute using the fundamental theorem of calculus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma}}\left|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}\frac{g(t-r;\overline{\gamma})-g(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})}{|\overline{\gamma}-\gamma_{n}^{*}|}dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>e_{n}\Big)

\displaystyle=\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma}}\left|\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}\frac{2C_{n,ij}^{*}}{nT}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}\frac{\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})ds(\overline{\gamma}-\gamma_{n}^{*})}{|\overline{\gamma}-\gamma_{n}^{*}|}dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>e_{n}\Bigg)}$}

\displaystyle\leq\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\underset{\sigma\in\{-1,1\}}{\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma}}}\left|\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{2\sigma\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})dsdN_{n,j}(r)}{nT}dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>e_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 284.52756pt+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}^{c})

\displaystyle\leq\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma}}\left|\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{2\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})dsdN_{n,j}(r)}{nT}dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>e_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)}$}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma}}\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\Big[\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;sP_{n}(\overline{\gamma})+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})ds\Big]dN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\Bigg|>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

(64)

\displaystyle+\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma,n,\eta}}\left|\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{2\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})dsdN_{n,j}(r)}{nT}dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 284.52756pt+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}^{c}).

(65)

We study (
65
) by using the union bound

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma,n,\eta}}\left|\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{2\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})dsdN_{n,j}(r)}{nT}dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)}

\displaystyle\leq\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle{\frac{K_{1}}{\eta}\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma,n,\eta}}\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\left|\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{2\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})dsdN_{n,j}(r)}{nT}dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg).}$}

(66)

The above can be handled by using Theorem 3 in
Hansen et al. (2015)
in its multivariate version. We let to this end,

H_{i}(t):=\frac{4}{nT}\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})dsdN_{n,j}(r)\mathbbm{1}(t\leq\tau_{n}).

By definition of
\tau_{n}
, the integral condition is fulfilled. Moreover,

\sup_{i=1,...,n}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau_{n}]}|H_{i}(t)|\leq\frac{4L_{g}\mathcal{N}\sup_{i=1,...,n}\|C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\|_{1}}{nT}\leq\frac{4L_{g}\mathcal{N}c_{n}}{nT}=:B.

Define next for
x=(1+\alpha_{4})\log nT

\displaystyle{\widehat{V}_{e}^{\mu}:=\frac{\mu}{\mu-\phi(\mu)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\tau_{n}}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\frac{4\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})dsdN_{n,j}(r)}{nT}\right)^{2}dN_{n,i}(t)}

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928pt+\scalebox{0.95}{$\displaystyle{\frac{16L_{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}c_{n}^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))n^{2}T^{2}}.}$}

On
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
it holds that
\tau_{n}=T
and hence

\displaystyle w:

\displaystyle=\frac{16L_{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}c_{n}^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))n^{2}T^{2}}\leq\widehat{V}_{e}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu}{\mu-\phi(\mu)}\frac{16L_{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{3}(T+A)}{An^{2}T^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\|_{1}^{2}+\frac{16L_{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}c_{n}^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))n^{2}T^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mu}{\mu-\phi(\mu)}\frac{16L_{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{3}(T+A)c_{n}^{2}}{AnT^{2}}+\frac{16L_{g}^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}c_{n}^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))n^{2}T^{2}}=:v.

Thus, Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
implies for
\varepsilon=1

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{2\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})dsdN_{n,j}(r)}{nT}dM_{n,i}(t)>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\tau_{n}}H_{i}(t)dM_{n,i}(t)>2\sqrt{\widehat{V}_{e}^{\mu}x}+\frac{Bx}{3},w\leq\widehat{V}_{e}^{\mu}\leq v,\sup_{i=1,...,n}\sup_{t\in[0,\tau_{n}]}|H_{i}(t)|\leq B\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu\mathcal{N}(T+A)n}{Ax}+1\right)+1\right)e^{-x}\leq c_{4}^{\prime}\log(nT)(nT)^{-(1+\alpha_{4})}

for a suitable constant
c_{4}^{\prime}
. Using this in (
66
) yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma,n,\eta}}\left|\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{2\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})dsdN_{n,j}(r)}{nT}dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\leq 2K_{1}\eta^{-1}c_{4}\log(nT)(nT)^{-(1+\alpha_{4})}\leq c_{4}\frac{\log(nT)\left(1+c_{n}\right)}{(nT)^{\alpha_{4}}}

for a suitable
c_{4}
.

For (
64
), we again make the convention
d|M_{n,i}|(t)=dN_{n,i}(t)+\lambda_{n,i}(t)dt
. We then get,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sup_{\overline{\gamma}\in K_{\gamma}}\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;s\overline{\gamma}+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{d}{d\gamma}g(t-r;sP_{n}(\overline{\gamma})+(1-s)\gamma_{n}^{*})dsdN_{n,j}(r)dM_{n,i}(t)\Bigg|>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{D_{g}\mathcal{N}}{nT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{T}d|M_{n,i}|(t)\eta>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\frac{D_{g}\mathcal{N}}{nT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}dN_{n,i}(t)

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\alpha_{n,i}\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{n}^{*})+\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{n,j}(r)dt\Bigg)\eta>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{D_{g}\mathcal{N}}{nT}\sum_{i,j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\left(\frac{T+A}{A}\mathcal{N}+T\left(K_{\alpha}\overline{\nu}_{i}+\|C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\overline{g}\mathcal{N}\right)\right)\eta>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{D_{g}\mathcal{N}}{nT}\left(\|C_{n}^{*}\|_{1}\left(\frac{T+A}{A}\mathcal{N}+TK_{\alpha}\|\overline{\nu}\|_{\infty}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|C_{n,i\cdot}^{*}\|_{1}^{2}\overline{g}\mathcal{N}T\right)\eta>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{D_{g}\mathcal{N}c_{n}}{T}\left(\frac{T+A}{A}\mathcal{N}+TK_{\alpha}\|\overline{\nu}\|_{\infty}+c_{n}\overline{g}\mathcal{N}T\right)\eta>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(c_{4}^{\prime\prime}c_{n}\left(1+c_{n}\right)\log^{2}(nT)\cdot\eta>\frac{e_{n}}{2},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right),

where the last inequality holds by definition of
c_{4}^{\prime\prime}
. But
\eta
is chosen such that the above probability equals zero.

Appendix H
Proofs of Section
3.2

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.

Since,
R_{n,ab}=\Sigma_{n,ab}(C,\alpha,\theta)
for some intermediate parameters that are the same within each row of
R_{n}
, we may ignore their dependence on the row and may simply study the row-wise difference between
\Sigma_{n}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})
and
\Sigma_{n}(C,\alpha,\theta)
for a parameter
(C,\alpha,\theta)
that lies between
(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})
and
(C_{2},\alpha_{2},\theta_{2})
. Recall that the entries of matrix
\Sigma_{n}
(and thus the entries of
R_{n}
) are of the form

\displaystyle\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\textrm{LS}_{i}(C,\alpha,\theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{T}2\partial_{a}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C,\alpha,\theta)\partial_{b}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C,\alpha,\theta)+2\Psi_{n,i}(t;C,\alpha,\beta)\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C,\alpha,\theta)dt

\displaystyle\qquad-2\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{a}\partial_{b}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C,\alpha,\theta)dN_{n,i}(t),

where
a
and
b
run through all the parameters. Also recall the definition of
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
from Lemma
B.2
.

Proving the upper bound from the lemma is tedious but straight-forward. In order to focus on the rates, we let
K
denote a constant that may change from line to line. It might depend on all constants mentioned in the lemma but not on
n
. We use the expressions for the derivatives of
\Psi_{n,i}
that were computed at the beginning of the chapter and the fact that many second derivatives vanish.

On
\Omega_{n}
, we have by using Assumptions (A1)-(A3) that for some constant
K>0
,

\displaystyle\left|\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})-\Psi_{n,i}(t;C,\alpha,\theta)\right|

\displaystyle=\Big|\alpha_{1,i}\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{1})-\alpha_{i}\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{t-}\left(C_{1,ij}g(t-r;\gamma_{1})-C_{ij}g(t-r;\gamma)\right)dN_{n,j}(r)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq K\big(|\alpha_{1,i}-\alpha_{i}|+\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1}+\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}-C_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\mathcal{N}+|\gamma_{1}-\gamma|\mathcal{N}\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\big),

and

\displaystyle\left|\Psi_{n,i}(t;C,\alpha,\theta)\right|\leq K\left(1+\|C_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\mathcal{N}\right).

Denote by
\nu_{0,r}
the derivative of
\nu_{0}
with respect to
\beta_{r}
, and by
\nu_{0,rq}
the second derivative of
\nu_{0}
with respect to
\beta_{r}
and
\beta_{q}
. In the following, we will bound the expression inside the maximum from the formulation the lemma, assuming that
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
holds, for each possible choice of
a
and
b
. Since
\Sigma
is symmetric and the arguments do not depend on the specifically chosen intermediate point, we may restrict to the upper triangular matrix.

Let
a=\alpha_{k}
and
b=\alpha_{l}
for some
k,l\in\{1,...,n\}
:

We will give a more detailed argument in this case.
Note that by the Assumptions (A1) and (A3), we have that

\displaystyle\left|\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{1})^{2}-\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{1})-\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta)\right|\cdot\left|\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{1})+\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta)\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\|\overline{\nu}\|_{\infty}L_{\nu}\cdot\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{2}\leq 2\|\overline{\nu}\|_{\infty}L_{\nu}C\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1},

where
C
is the universal constant for which
\|x\|_{2}\leq C\|x\|_{1}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Then, with
K:=2\|\overline{\nu}\|_{\infty}L_{\nu}C
we obtain

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{2}{nT}\int_{0}^{T}\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{1})^{2}-\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta)^{2}dt\mathbbm{1}(k=l)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1}.

Let
a=\alpha_{k}
and
b=C_{xy}
for some
k,x,y\in\{1,...,n\}
:

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\right|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\int_{0}^{T}\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{1})\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,y}(r)

\displaystyle\qquad-\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta)\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,y}(r)dt\mathbbm{1}(x=k)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1}+\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma|.

Let
a=\alpha_{k}
and
b=\beta_{r}
for some
k\in\{1,...,n\}
and
r\in\{1,...,p\}
:

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\right|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\Big[\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{1})\alpha_{1,k}\nu_{0,r}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{1})-\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta)\alpha_{k}\nu_{0,r}(X_{k}(t);\beta)\Big]dt

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\Big[\Psi_{n,k}(t;C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\nu_{0,r}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{1})-\Psi_{n,k}(t;C,\alpha,\theta)\nu_{0,r}(X_{k}(t);\beta)\Big]dt

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{0}^{T}\Big[\nu_{0,r}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{1})-\nu_{0,r}(X_{k}(t);\beta)\Big]dN_{n,k}(t)\Bigg)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\mathcal{N}(1+\|C_{1,k\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1})}{n}\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1}+\frac{K}{n}|\alpha_{1,k}-\alpha_{k}|+\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}\|C_{1,k\cdot}-C_{k\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}+\frac{K\mathcal{N}\|C_{1,k\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}}{n}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma|

Let
a=\alpha_{k}
and
b=\gamma
:

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\right|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\int_{0}^{T}\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{1})\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{1,kj}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r);\gamma_{1})dN_{n,j}(r)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt-\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta)\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{kj}\int_{0}^{t-1}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,j}(r)dt\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\|C_{1,k\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\mathcal{N}}{n}\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1}+\frac{K\|C_{1,k\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\mathcal{N}}{n}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma|+\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}\|C_{1,k\boldsymbol{\cdot}}-C_{k\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}.

Let
a=C_{xy}
and
b=C_{x^{\prime}y^{\prime}}
for some
x,y,x^{\prime},y^{\prime}\in\{1,...,n\}
:

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\right|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,y}(r)\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,y^{\prime}}(r)

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,y}(r)\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,y^{\prime}}(r)dt\mathbbm{1}(x=x^{\prime})\Bigg|\leq\frac{K\mathcal{N}^{2}}{n}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma|

Let
a=C_{xy}
and
b=\beta_{r}
for some
x,y\in\{1,...,n\}
and
r\in\{1,...,p\}
:

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\right|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,y}(r)\alpha_{1,x}\nu_{0,r}(X_{x}(t);\beta_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,y}(r)\alpha_{x}\nu_{0,r}(X_{x}(t);\beta)dt\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma|+\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}|\alpha_{1,x}-\alpha_{x}|+\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1}

Let
a=C_{xy}
and
b=\gamma
for some
x,y\in\{1,...,n\}
:

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\right|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,y}(r)\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{1,xj}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma_{1})\Big]dN_{n,j}(r)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,y}(r)\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{xj}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma)\Big]dN_{n,j}(r)dt

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\Psi_{n,x}(t;C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,y}(r)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad-\Psi_{n,x}(t;C,\alpha,\theta)\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,y}(r)dt

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,y}(r)-\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,y}(r)dN_{n,x}(t)\Bigg)\Bigg|\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\mathcal{N}^{2}(1+\|C_{1,x\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1})}{n}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma|+\frac{K\mathcal{N}^{2}}{n}\|C_{1,x\boldsymbol{\cdot}}-C_{x\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}+\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}|\alpha_{1,x}-\alpha_{x}|+\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1}

Let
a=\beta_{r}
and
b=\beta_{q}
for some
r,q\in\{1,...,p\}
:

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\right|

\displaystyle=\scalebox{0.95}{$\displaystyle{\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg(\int_{0}^{T}\Big[\alpha_{1,i}^{2}\nu_{0,r}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{1})\nu_{0,q}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{1})-\alpha_{i}^{2}\nu_{0,r}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta)\Big]\nu_{0,q}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta)dt}$}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\Big[\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\alpha_{1,i}\nu_{0,rq}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{1})-\Psi_{n,i}(t;C,\alpha,\theta)\alpha_{i}\nu_{0,rq}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta)\Big]dt

\displaystyle\qquad-\int_{0}^{T}\Big[\alpha_{1,i}\nu_{0,rq}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{1})-\alpha_{i}\nu_{0,rq}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta)\Big]dN_{n,i}(t)\Bigg)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K(1+\max_{i=1,...,n}\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1})\mathcal{N}}{n}\|\alpha_{1}-\alpha\|_{1}+K\left(1+\frac{1}{n}\|C_{1}\|_{1}\right)\mathcal{N}\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}\|C_{1}-C\|_{1}+\frac{K\mathcal{N}\|C_{1}\|_{1}}{n}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma|

Let
a=\beta_{r}
and
b=\gamma
for some
r\in\{1,...,p\}
:

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\right|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\alpha_{1,i}\nu_{0,r}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{1})\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{1,ij}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,j}(r)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt-\alpha_{i}\nu_{0,r}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta)\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{ij}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,j}(r)dt\Bigg|\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K}{n}\max_{i=1,...,n}\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\mathcal{N}\|\alpha_{1}-\alpha\|_{1}+\frac{K\|C_{1}\|_{1}}{n}\mathcal{N}\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1}+\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{n}\|C_{1}-C\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{K\|C_{1}\|_{1}}{n}\mathcal{N}|\gamma_{1}-\gamma|

Let
a=\gamma
and
b=\gamma
:

\displaystyle\left|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\right|

\displaystyle=\scalebox{0.9}{$\displaystyle{\Bigg|\frac{2}{nT}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{T}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{1,ij}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,j}(r)\Big)^{2}-\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{ij}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,j}(r)\Big)^{2}dt}$}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int_{0}^{T}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{1,ij}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime\prime}(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,j}(r)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\Psi_{n,i}(t;C,\alpha,\theta)\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{ij}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime\prime}(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,j}(r)dt

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{1,ij}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime\prime}(t-r;\gamma_{1})dN_{n,j}(r)-\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{ij}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime\prime}(t-r;\gamma)dN_{n,j}(r)dN_{n,i}(t)\Bigg]\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\mathcal{N}\max_{i=1,...,n}(1,\mathcal{N}\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1},\mathcal{N}\|C_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1})}{n}\|C_{1}-C\|_{1}

\displaystyle+K\mathcal{N}^{2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\max(\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1},\|C_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1})|\gamma_{1}-\gamma|+\frac{K\mathcal{N}\max_{i=1,...,n}\|C_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}}{n}\|\alpha_{1}-\alpha\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+K\mathcal{N}\frac{1}{n}\|C\|_{1}\|\beta_{1}-\beta\|_{1}.

Combining all of these statements, we find that that on
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
, recalling that
(C,\alpha,\theta)
lies between
(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})
and
(C_{2},\alpha_{2},\theta_{2})
,

\displaystyle\max_{a,b}\big|R_{n,ab}-\Sigma_{n,ab}(C_{1},\alpha_{1},\theta_{1})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K\mathcal{N}\left(\max_{i=1,...,n}(\|C_{2,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1},\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1})+1\right)}{n}\|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{K\mathcal{N}^{2}\max_{i=1,...,n}(1,\|C_{2,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1},\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1})}{n}\|C_{1}-C_{2}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+K\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{1}{n}\max(\|C_{1}\|_{1},\|C_{2}\|_{1})+1\right)\|\beta_{1}-\beta_{2}\|_{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{K\mathcal{N}^{2}}{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}\max(\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1},\|C_{2,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1},1)+1\right)|\gamma_{1}-\gamma_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq K\mathcal{N}^{2}\max_{i=1,...,n}(\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1},\|C_{2,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1},1)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\Big(\frac{1}{n}\|\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{2}\|_{1}+\frac{1}{n}\|C_{1}-C_{2}\|_{1}+\Big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|C_{1,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\|_{1}+1\Big)\|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}\|_{1}\Big)

Since
K
does not depend on
n
explicitly, the above bound holds uniformly. Recalling that for
\mathcal{N}\approx\log(nT)
, Lemma
B.2
provides
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{N}})\to 1
, the statement follows.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.

Recall the definition of
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
from Lemma
B.2
for
\mathcal{N}=\mathcal{N}_{0}\log(nT)
with a suitable
\mathcal{N}_{0}>0
. By a union bound argument, we have for any sequence

\varepsilon_{n}:=c_{0}c_{n}\log(nT)

(
c_{0}
will be chosen below),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\Bigg\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\theta}\\
\partial_{\alpha}\\
\partial_{C}\end{pmatrix}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\cdot}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\Bigg\|_{\infty}>\varepsilon_{n}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq n^{2}\max_{x,y=1,...,n}\mathbb{P}\left(\,\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{C_{xy}}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\varepsilon_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

(67)

\displaystyle+n\max_{k=1,...,n}\mathbb{P}\left(\,\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{\alpha_{k}}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\varepsilon_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

(68)

\displaystyle+p\max_{r=1,...,p}\mathbb{P}\left(\,\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{\beta_{r}}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\varepsilon_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

(69)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left(\,\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{\gamma}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\varepsilon_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)+\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}^{c}).

(70)

Since
\mathbb{P}(\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}^{c})\to 0
by Lemma
B.2
, we have left to prove that the remaining probabilities converge to zero. All expressions above can be handled using Theorem 3 in
Hansen et al. (2015)
and using the computations of the derivatives of
\Psi_{n,i}
from the beginning of this chapter. To this end, fix
\mu\in(0,3)
as in Theorem 3 in
Hansen et al. (2015)
(cf. also
\mu
from Lemma
C.1
). Let furthermore
\tau_{n}
be defined as in the proof of Lemma
C.1
. For (
67
), we define

H_{i}(t):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\partial_{C_{xy}}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{n,y}(r)\mathbbm{1}(i=x)\mathbbm{1}(t\leq\tau_{n})

We see that only
H_{x}
is different from zero. We may therefore set
M=1
in Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
. It holds that
|H_{x}(t)|\leq\frac{K\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{nT}}:=B
for some constant
K
and the integral condition holds. We then define for
x=c\log(nT)
(
c>2
)

\widehat{V}^{\mu}:=\frac{\mu}{\mu-\phi(\mu)}\int_{0}^{\tau_{n}}\frac{1}{nT}\left(\int_{0}^{t-}g(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{n,y}(r)\right)^{2}dN_{n,x}(t)+\frac{K^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}.

On
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
, it holds that
\tau_{n}=T
and furthermore for a suitable
K^{\prime}>0

w:=\frac{K^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}\leq\widehat{V}^{\mu}\leq\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}^{3}}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nA}+\frac{K^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}=:v.

Note now that on
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
for
n
large enough and a suitable constant
K^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\widehat{V}^{\mu}x}+\frac{K\mathcal{N}x}{3\sqrt{nT}}\leq K^{\prime\prime}\frac{\log^{2}nT}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\varepsilon_{n}.

We then have by Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
using
\varepsilon=1

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{C_{xy}}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\varepsilon_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau_{n}}H_{x}(t)dM_{n,x}(t)>2\sqrt{\widehat{V}^{\mu}x}+\frac{K\mathcal{N}x}{3\sqrt{nT}},w\leq\widehat{V}^{\mu}\leq v,\sup_{t\in[0,\tau_{n}]}|H_{x}(t)|\leq B\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}T}{AK^{2}x}+1\right)+1\right)e^{-x}=4\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}_{0}T}{AK^{2}c}+1\right)+1\right)(nT)^{-c}.

Since
c>2
, the above implies that (
67
) converges to zero. The argument for (
68
) goes similarly: Define

H_{i}(t):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\partial_{\alpha_{k}}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\nu_{0}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{n}^{*})\mathbbm{1}(i=k).

Thus, only
H_{k}
is different from zero and we may again use
M=1
in Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
. It holds that
|H_{k}(t)|\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{nT}}:=B
for some constant
K
for all
t\in[0,T]
. The integral condition of Theorem 3 is hence fulfilled. We then define

\widehat{V}^{\mu}:=\frac{\mu}{\mu-\phi(\mu)}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{nT}\nu_{0}(X_{k}(t);\beta_{n}^{*})^{2}dN_{n,k}(t)+\frac{K^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}.

On
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
it holds for a constant
K^{\prime}>0
,

w:=\frac{K^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}\leq\widehat{V}^{\mu}\leq\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nA}+\frac{K^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}=:v.

On
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
, for
n
large enough and for a suitable constant
K^{\prime\prime}
,

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\widehat{V}^{\mu}x}+\frac{Kx}{3\sqrt{nT}}\leq K^{\prime\prime}\frac{\log nT}{\sqrt{n}}\leq\varepsilon_{n}.

We then have by Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
using
\varepsilon=1

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{\alpha_{k}}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\right|>\varepsilon_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{P}\left(\int_{0}^{\tau_{n}}H_{k}(t)dM_{n,k}(t)>2\sqrt{\widehat{V}^{\mu}x}+\frac{Kx}{3\sqrt{nT}},w\leq\widehat{V}^{\mu}\leq v,\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|H_{k}(t)|\leq B\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}T}{AK^{2}x}+1\right)+1\right)e^{-x}=4\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}_{0}T}{AK^{2}c}+1\right)+1\right)(nT)^{-c}.

Hence, (
68
) converges to zero because
c>2
. We study now (
69
). The proof follows the same ideas. Recall that we denote the first derivative of
\nu_{0}
with respect to
\beta_{r}
by
\nu_{0,r}
. Let

H_{i}(t):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\partial_{\beta_{r}}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\alpha_{n,i}^{*}\nu_{0,r}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{n}^{*}).

This time all
H_{i}
are different from zero and we use the multivariate version of Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
, that is,
M=n
. It holds that
|H_{i}(t)|\leq\frac{K}{\sqrt{nT}}:=B
for some constant
K
for all
t\in[0,T]
. The integral condition of Theorem 3 is hence fulfilled. We then define

\widehat{V}^{\mu}:=\frac{\mu}{\mu-\phi(\mu)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\frac{1}{nT}(\alpha_{n,i}^{*})^{2}\nu_{0,r}(X_{n,i}(t);\beta_{n}^{*})^{2}dN_{n,i}(t)+\frac{K^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}.

On
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
it holds for a constant
K^{\prime}>0
,

w:=\frac{K^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}\leq\widehat{V}^{\mu}\leq\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}\|\alpha_{n}^{*}\|_{1}}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nA}+\frac{K^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}=:v.

On
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
for
n
large enough and a suitable constant
K^{\prime\prime}
, and a good choice of
c_{0}

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\widehat{V}^{\mu}x}+\frac{Kx}{3\sqrt{nT}}\leq K^{\prime\prime}\log nT\leq\varepsilon_{n}.

We then have by Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
using
\varepsilon=1

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{\beta_{r}}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\Big|>\varepsilon_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}H_{i}(t)dM_{n,i}(t)>2\sqrt{\widehat{V}^{\mu}x}+\frac{Kx}{3\sqrt{nT}},w\leq\widehat{V}^{\mu}\leq v,\underset{i=1,...,n}{\sup_{t\in[0,T]}}|H_{i}(t)|\leq B\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 4\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}\|\alpha_{n}^{*}\|_{1}T}{AK^{2}x}+1\right)+1\right)e^{-x}=4\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}_{0}\|\alpha_{n}^{*}\|_{1}T}{AK^{2}c}+1\right)+1\right)(nT)^{-c}.

The above implies that (
69
) converges to zero because
\|\alpha_{n}^{*}\|_{1}=O(n)
. Finally, we study (
70
). Let this time

H_{i}(t):=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\partial_{\gamma}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{n,j}(r)\mathbbm{1}(t\leq\tau_{n}).

Again, all
H_{i}
are different from zero and we use
M=n
in Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
. We have
|H_{i}(t)|\leq\frac{KS\mathcal{N}}{\sqrt{nT}}:=B
for
S:=(1\vee\max_{i=1,...,n}\|C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*}\|_{1})
and some constant
K
and hence the integral condition of Theorem 3 is fulfilled. Define

\displaystyle\widehat{V}^{\mu}:=\frac{\mu}{\mu-\phi(\mu)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\tau_{n}}\frac{1}{nT}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}C_{n,ij}^{*}\int_{0}^{t-}g^{\prime}(t-r;\gamma_{n}^{*})dN_{n,j}(r)\right)^{2}dN_{n,i}(t)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{K^{2}S^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}.

On
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
it holds for a constant
K^{\prime}>0
,

w:=\frac{K^{2}S^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}\leq\widehat{V}^{\mu}\leq\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}^{3}S^{2}}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))A}+\frac{K^{2}S^{2}\mathcal{N}^{2}x}{(\mu-\phi(\mu))nT}=:v.

On
\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}
for
n
large enough and a suitable constant
K^{\prime\prime}
and after possibly increasing
c_{0}

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{\widehat{V}^{\mu}x}+\frac{KS\mathcal{N}x}{3\sqrt{nT}}\leq K^{\prime\prime}S\log nT\leq\varepsilon_{n}.

We then have by Theorem 3 of
Hansen et al. (2015)
using
\varepsilon=1

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\Big|\frac{1}{\sqrt{nT}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{\gamma}\Psi_{n,i}(t;C_{n,i\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*},\alpha_{n,i}^{*},\theta_{n}^{*})dM_{n,i}(t)\Big|>\varepsilon_{n},\Omega_{\mathcal{N}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{0}^{\tau_{n}}H_{i}(t)dM_{n,i}(t)>2\sqrt{\widehat{V}^{\mu}x}+\frac{KS\mathcal{N}x}{3\sqrt{nT}},w\leq\widehat{V}^{\mu}\leq v,\underset{i=1,...,n}{\sup_{t\in[0,T]}}|H_{i}(t)|\leq B\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq 4\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}nT}{AK^{2}x}+1\right)+1\right)e^{-x}=4\left(\log_{2}\left(\frac{\mu K^{\prime}\mathcal{N}_{0}nT}{AK^{2}c}+1\right)+1\right)(nT)^{-c},

which converges to zero. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Appendix I
Proofs of Section
3.3

Proof of Theorem
3.8
.

The proof runs exactly along the lines of the proof of Theorem 6.2 in
Bühlmann and van de Geer (2011)
. However, for completeness we repeat it here in our setting. We write

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{i}(C,\alpha,\theta):

\displaystyle=\|\Psi_{n,i}(\cdot;C_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}},\alpha_{i},\theta)-\lambda_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\lambda_{n,i}(\cdot)\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\textrm{LS}_{i}(C_{i\boldsymbol{\cdot}},\alpha_{i},\theta)+2\int_{0}^{T}\Psi_{n,i}(\cdot;C_{i\cdot},\alpha_{i},\theta)dM_{i}(t),

so that
\mathcal{E}(C,\nu,\theta)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{E}_{i}(C,\nu,\theta).
We have
